Um… with one hand shaking while I’m using it? I don’t think I’ve noticed that, not really.

Like, I don’t really watch my arms when I walk, you know? So… I’m not sure, but I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements in general feel slower than they used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I could get into a rhythm. Now I’ll be standing there trying to part the hair straight and it’s like my hands just won’t catch up with what I’m thinking. I’m not sure. I don’t think so, but I’d have to look at old notes or something.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any stiffness or rigidity in your arms or legs?\n\nPatient: Stiffness… um, I don’t know. shampoo stations saying I’m ruining everything, even when the salon is empty or nobody is close by.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: I used to chat with clients, help the newer stylists, and go hiking with friends, but now I mostly keep my head down and avoid people because I just don’t have that spark in me.\n- key: S024\n  name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest, like there’s no point in trying to build the life I was planning.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: I’ve been skipping meals without really meaning to, then realizing at night I only had coffee and a few bites of something, and my clothes have gotten looser.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I’m a burden on everyone and that I deserve what’s happening because I couldn’t keep my money, my job, or even basic salon responsibilities together.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I stare at color formulas or the appointment screen and my brain just blanks, so I second-guess simple things I used to do automatically.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don’t have a plan to hurt myself, but I get thoughts like it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up and nobody had to deal with me anymore.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When the sadness got heavy and stayed, the voice at the shampoo stations and the belief that someone was sabotaging my bookings were happening at the same time, not separately.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, I have spent most of this whole decline feeling depressed and weighed down, with only shorter stretches where the sadness was not as prominent.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: over, there was a stretch of several weeks when I was still getting up and functioning more normally, but I was already hearing the voice and feeling sure someone at the salon was interfering with my schedule.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I have lost trust from my manager and clients because I make mistakes with basic salon routines, work too slowly, and cancel things I used to reliably handle. But then my savings started draining away, and I’d check my account and feel like I was just watching everything I worked for disappear.\n\nThat was… maybe about a year and eight months ago now? Around then is when it really started feeling like this instead of just regular stress.\n\nDoctor: When you have a day that your money situation feels less stressful, does your mood improve noticeably?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe I’ll feel a tiny bit less panicky if, like, a bill isn’t due that exact day, but the mood doesn’t really lift.\n\nMost days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness sitting in my chest, like… like there’s no point in trying to build the life I thought I was going to have. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I came in because things have been feeling off, like I’m not keeping up the way I used to. At work, my hands feel like they’re moving through glue or something. I’m a hairdresser, so I’m usually pretty quick with sectioning hair or mixing color, like that’s just muscle memory, you know? But lately it takes me forever.\n\nAnd it’s embarrassing because people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be. Like clients don’t always say it directly, but you can tell, and coworkers… I don’t know. It makes me feel weird. I used to be good at getting through appointments without thinking so hard about every little step.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any shaking or tremor in your hands when they’re resting?\n\nPatient: Um… resting? I don’t think so. Like if my hands are just in my lap, I haven’t noticed them shaking or anything. I mean, sometimes I stare at them because I’m worried they’re not doing what I want fast enough, but that’s more when I’m working, like holding scissors or those little foils that stick to everything. But just sitting there… no, I don’t think I’ve seen a tremor.\n\nDoctor: Have your muscles been feeling stiff?\n\nPatient: Stiff… um, I’m not sure. I don’t really think of it as stiff, exactly. Like sometimes after standing all day at the salon I feel kind of tight, but that could just be normal, you know? We have those mats but they’re not as comfortable as people think, and then you end up leaning weird over the chair when someone’s hair is really long.\n\nBut muscle stiffness like… I don’t know, I haven’t really noticed that in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: When you walk, have you noticed one arm swinging less than the other?\n\nPatient: I… don’t think I’ve noticed that. Like, I don’t really watch my arms when I walk, you know? I’m usually thinking about where I’m going, or if I left my coffee somewhere at the salon, or whether I’m late.\n\nBut no one’s said anything to me about one arm not swinging, and I haven’t caught it in a mirror or anything. So… I’m not sure, but I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements in general feel slower than they used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to explain, but at work it’s like my hands are moving through glue or something. Like sectioning hair, mixing color, even folding the foils… it all takes me way longer than it used to.\n\nAnd I used to be pretty fast, you know, like that was kind of my thing. I could get into a rhythm. Now I’ll be standing there trying to part the hair straight and it’s like my hands just won’t catch up with what I’m thinking. People have noticed too—like coworkers kind of waiting on me, or clients asking if everything’s okay. So… yeah, I feel slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your handwriting getting smaller than it used to be?\n\nPatient: My handwriting? Um… I don’t know, I haven’t really noticed it getting smaller. I don’t write by hand that much anymore, honestly—mostly I’m typing appointments into the system or texting clients, and at the salon it’s all little notes on color formulas, which are already messy because I’m rushing and there’s foil everywhere and gloves on and—yeah.\n\nSo… smaller? I’m not sure. I don’t think so, but I’d have to look at old notes or something.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any stiffness or rigidity in your arms or legs?\n\nPatient: Stiffness… um, I don’t know. Like, my legs get tired if I’m standing at the salon all day, but that’s kind of normal, right? Everybody complains about that—especially when we’re booked back to back and someone’s doing a full color correction and you barely sit down.\n\nBut rigidity, like… locked up? I haven’t really noticed that. My arms and legs don’t feel stiff in a way that I can clearly point to. I guess I’m not totally sure what you mean by rigidity, but nothing obvious like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed one hand shaking when you’re using it, like while cutting hair or holding something?\n\nPatient: Um… with one hand shaking while I’m using it? I don’t think I’ve noticed that, not really.\n\nI mean, sometimes my hands feel awkward when I’m cutting or holding a brush, but I always think that’s because I’m trying to hurry, or because the client is talking and I’m trying to listen and count the sections in my head—like if I lose track of where I put the clips, then everything gets messy. But an actual shake, like one hand trembling? I can’t say I’ve seen that. If it happened, I don’t think it’s been obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I wake up and it’s like this heavy, flat sadness just sitting in my chest, and before anything even happens I’m already tired of the day. Like there’s no point trying to build the life I was planning, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd then I’ll start thinking about stupid stuff too, like how I was supposed to have a nicer apartment by now, or a better station at work, or—sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked. But yeah, depressed most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I used to actually like talking with clients, like hearing about their vacations or their kid’s dance recital or whatever, and I’d help the newer stylists figure things out when they got overwhelmed.\n\nNow I mostly just keep my head down and get through the appointment. Like, I don’t want to be rude, but I don’t have that… spark, I guess. Even hiking with my friends, which I used to love—there’s this trail we’d do where there’s a little overlook and everyone takes pictures, and now when they ask, I just don’t go. I tell myself I’m tired or busy, but really I just don’t feel much from it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like times where I felt really down or I didn’t want to deal with people, but I don’t know if it was most days for two weeks or if I’m just thinking of, like, being stressed.\n\nIt’s hard for me to separate it, because I’ll remember one thing—like being upset about work or feeling behind compared to everyone else—and then I start thinking about all these other periods in my life, and they kind of blur together. So… I don’t know. I can’t give you a clear yes or no.\n\nDoctor: Did these symptoms start after a specific stressful event in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it did start after something specific. My hours got cut at the salon, and at first I kept telling myself it was temporary, like everyone has slow weeks, you know? Around then is when it really started feeling like this instead of just regular stress.\n\nDoctor: When you have a day that your money situation feels less stressful, does your mood improve noticeably?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe I’ll feel a tiny bit less panicky if, like, a bill isn’t due that exact day, but the mood doesn’t really lift.\n\nMost days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness sitting in my chest, like… like there’s no point in trying to build the life I thought I was going to have. Even if the money stuff is quieter for a day, that feeling is still there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this depressed nearly every day for at least two months in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely more than two months. Pretty much nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Before this current depression, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if there was a whole separate time before this like that. Like sectioning hair, mixing color, even folding the foils… it all takes me way longer than it used to.\n\nAnd I used to be pretty fast, you know, like that was kind of my thing. I could get into a rhythm. Now I’ll be standing there trying to part the hair straight and it’s like my hands just won’t catch up with what I’m thinking. People have noticed too—like coworkers kind of waiting on me, or clients asking if everything’s okay. So… yeah, I feel slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your handwriting getting smaller than it used to be?\n\nPatient: My handwriting? Um… I don’t know, I haven’t really noticed it getting smaller. I don’t write by hand that much anymore, honestly—mostly I’m typing appointments into the system or texting clients, and at the salon it’s all little notes on color formulas, which are already messy because I’m rushing and there’s foil everywhere and gloves on and—yeah.\n\nSo… smaller? I’m not sure. I don’t think so, but I’d have to look at old notes or something.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any stiffness or rigidity in your arms or legs?\n\nPatient: Stiffness… um, I don’t know. Like, my legs get tired if I’m standing at the salon all day, but that’s kind of normal, right? Everybody complains about that—especially when we’re booked back to back and someone’s doing a full color correction and you barely sit down.\n\nBut rigidity, like… locked up? I haven’t really noticed that. My arms and legs don’t feel stiff in a way that I can clearly point to. I guess I’m not totally sure what you mean by rigidity, but nothing obvious like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed one hand shaking when you’re using it, like while cutting hair or holding something?\n\nPatient: Um… with one hand shaking while I’m using it? I don’t think I’ve noticed that, not really.\n\nI mean, sometimes my hands feel awkward when I’m cutting or holding a brush, but I always think that’s because I’m trying to hurry, or because the client is talking and I’m trying to listen and count the sections in my head—like if I lose track of where I put the clips, then everything gets messy. But an actual shake, like one hand trembling? I can’t say I’ve seen that. If it happened, I don’t think it’s been obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I wake up and it’s like this heavy, flat sadness just sitting in my chest, and before anything even happens I’m already tired of the day. Like there’s no point trying to build the life I was planning, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd then I’ll start thinking about stupid stuff too, like how I was supposed to have a nicer apartment by now, or a better station at work, or—sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked. But yeah, depressed most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I used to actually like talking with clients, like hearing about their vacations or their kid’s dance recital or whatever, and I’d help the newer stylists figure things out when they got overwhelmed.\n\nNow I mostly just keep my head down and get through the appointment. Like, I don’t want to be rude, but I don’t have that… spark, I guess. Even hiking with my friends, which I used to love—there’s this trail we’d do where there’s a little overlook and everyone takes pictures, and now when they ask, I just don’t go. I tell myself I’m tired or busy, but really I just don’t feel much from it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like times where I felt really down or I didn’t want to deal with people, but I don’t know if it was most days for two weeks or if I’m just thinking of, like, being stressed.\n\nIt’s hard for me to separate it, because I’ll remember one thing—like being upset about work or feeling behind compared to everyone else—and then I start thinking about all these other periods in my life, and they kind of blur together. So… I don’t know. I can’t give you a clear yes or no.\n\nDoctor: Did these symptoms start after a specific stressful event in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it did start after something specific. My hours got cut at the salon, and at first I kept telling myself it was temporary, like everyone has slow weeks, you know? But then my savings started draining away, and I’d check my account and feel like I was just watching everything I worked for disappear.\n\nThat was… maybe about a year and eight months ago now? Around then is when it really started feeling like this instead of just regular stress.\n\nDoctor: When you have a day that your money situation feels less stressful, does your mood improve noticeably?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe I’ll feel a tiny bit less panicky if, like, a bill isn’t due that exact day, but the mood doesn’t really lift.\n\nMost days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness sitting in my chest, like… like there’s no point in trying to build the life I thought I was going to have. Even if the money stuff is quieter for a day, that feeling is still there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this depressed nearly every day for at least two months in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely more than two months. I mean, it’s hard to track exactly because the days kind of run together, and I’ll think, “Was that spring or last winter?” and then I start remembering random stuff from work and it gets mixed up.\n\nBut the really heavy depression… that’s been with me about a year and two months now, I think. Pretty much nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Before this current depression, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if there was a whole separate time before this like that. I remember having bad weeks, like after stuff with school or work, and I’d get really down, but this current thing feels different.\n\nMost days now I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest, like there’s no point in trying to build the life I was planning. Before this, I’m not sure it was ever that constant for two weeks straight… I’d have to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been forgetting important things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I don’t know if it’s exactly forgetting, but my brain just blanks out more than it used to.\n\nLike at work, I’ll be staring at a color formula or the appointment screen, and it’s something I’ve done a million times, but suddenly I’m second-guessing simple stuff. Then I start clicking around, and someone’s talking about their cousin’s wedding hair or whatever, and I’m like… wait, what was I even doing? It makes me feel stupid, honestly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t think so. Like if my hands are just in my lap, I haven’t noticed them shaking or anything. I mean, sometimes I stare at them because I’m worried they’re not doing what I want fast enough, but that’s more when I’m working, like holding scissors or those little foils that stick to everything. But just sitting there… no, I don’t think I’ve seen a tremor.\n\nDoctor: Have your muscles been feeling stiff?\n\nPatient: Stiff… um, I’m not sure. I don’t really think of it as stiff, exactly. Like sometimes after standing all day at the salon I feel kind of tight, but that could just be normal, you know? We have those mats but they’re not as comfortable as people think, and then you end up leaning weird over the chair when someone’s hair is really long.\n\nBut muscle stiffness like… I don’t know, I haven’t really noticed that in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: When you walk, have you noticed one arm swinging less than the other?\n\nPatient: I… don’t think I’ve noticed that. Like, I don’t really watch my arms when I walk, you know? I’m usually thinking about where I’m going, or if I left my coffee somewhere at the salon, or whether I’m late.\n\nBut no one’s said anything to me about one arm not swinging, and I haven’t caught it in a mirror or anything. So… I’m not sure, but I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements in general feel slower than they used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to explain, but at work it’s like my hands are moving through glue or something. Like sectioning hair, mixing color, even folding the foils… it all takes me way longer than it used to.\n\nAnd I used to be pretty fast, you know, like that was kind of my thing. I could get into a rhythm. Now I’ll be standing there trying to part the hair straight and it’s like my hands just won’t catch up with what I’m thinking. People have noticed too—like coworkers kind of waiting on me, or clients asking if everything’s okay. So… yeah, I feel slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your handwriting getting smaller than it used to be?\n\nPatient: My handwriting? Um… I don’t know, I haven’t really noticed it getting smaller. I don’t write by hand that much anymore, honestly—mostly I’m typing appointments into the system or texting clients, and at the salon it’s all little notes on color formulas, which are already messy because I’m rushing and there’s foil everywhere and gloves on and—yeah.\n\nSo… smaller? I’m not sure. I don’t think so, but I’d have to look at old notes or something.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any stiffness or rigidity in your arms or legs?\n\nPatient: Stiffness… um, I don’t know. Like, my legs get tired if I’m standing at the salon all day, but that’s kind of normal, right? Everybody complains about that—especially when we’re booked back to back and someone’s doing a full color correction and you barely sit down.\n\nBut rigidity, like… locked up? I haven’t really noticed that. My arms and legs don’t feel stiff in a way that I can clearly point to. I guess I’m not totally sure what you mean by rigidity, but nothing obvious like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed one hand shaking when you’re using it, like while cutting hair or holding something?\n\nPatient: Um… with one hand shaking while I’m using it? I don’t think I’ve noticed that, not really.\n\nI mean, sometimes my hands feel awkward when I’m cutting or holding a brush, but I always think that’s because I’m trying to hurry, or because the client is talking and I’m trying to listen and count the sections in my head—like if I lose track of where I put the clips, then everything gets messy. But an actual shake, like one hand trembling? I can’t say I’ve seen that. If it happened, I don’t think it’s been obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I wake up and it’s like this heavy, flat sadness just sitting in my chest, and before anything even happens I’m already tired of the day. Like there’s no point trying to build the life I was planning, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd then I’ll start thinking about stupid stuff too, like how I was supposed to have a nicer apartment by now, or a better station at work, or—sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked. But yeah, depressed most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I used to actually like talking with clients, like hearing about their vacations or their kid’s dance recital or whatever, and I’d help the newer stylists figure things out when they got overwhelmed.\n\nNow I mostly just keep my head down and get through the appointment. Like, I don’t want to be rude, but I don’t have that… spark, I guess. Even hiking with my friends, which I used to love—there’s this trail we’d do where there’s a little overlook and everyone takes pictures, and now when they ask, I just don’t go. I tell myself I’m tired or busy, but really I just don’t feel much from it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like times where I felt really down or I didn’t want to deal with people, but I don’t know if it was most days for two weeks or if I’m just thinking of, like, being stressed.\n\nIt’s hard for me to separate it, because I’ll remember one thing—like being upset about work or feeling behind compared to everyone else—and then I start thinking about all these other periods in my life, and they kind of blur together. So… I don’t know. I can’t give you a clear yes or no.\n\nDoctor: Did these symptoms start after a specific stressful event in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it did start after something specific. My hours got cut at the salon, and at first I kept telling myself it was temporary, like everyone has slow weeks, you know? But then my savings started draining away, and I’d check my account and feel like I was just watching everything I worked for disappear.\n\nThat was… maybe about a year and eight months ago now? Around then is when it really started feeling like this instead of just regular stress.\n\nDoctor: When you have a day that your money situation feels less stressful, does your mood improve noticeably?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe I’ll feel a tiny bit less panicky if, like, a bill isn’t due that exact day, but the mood doesn’t really lift.\n\nMost days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness sitting in my chest, like… like there’s no point in trying to build the life I thought I was going to have. Even if the money stuff is quieter for a day, that feeling is still there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this depressed nearly every day for at least two months in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely more than two months. I mean, it’s hard to track exactly because the days kind of run together, and I’ll think, “Was that spring or last winter?” and then I start remembering random stuff from work and it gets mixed up.\n\nBut the really heavy depression… that’s been with me about a year and two months now, I think. Pretty much nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Before this current depression, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if there was a whole separate time before this like that. I remember having bad weeks, like after stuff with school or work, and I’d get really down, but this current thing feels different.\n\nMost days now I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest, like there’s no point in trying to build the life I was planning. Before this, I’m not sure it was ever that constant for two weeks straight… I’d have to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been forgetting important things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I don’t know if it’s exactly forgetting, but my brain just blanks out more than it used to.\n\nLike at work, I’ll be staring at a color formula or the appointment screen, and it’s something I’ve done a million times, but suddenly I’m second-guessing simple stuff. Then I start clicking around, and someone’s talking about their cousin’s wedding hair or whatever, and I’m like… wait, what was I even doing? It makes me feel stupid, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that your face looks less expressive than it used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, people don’t always say “your face looks different,” but they’ll ask if I’m mad or if I’m tired, even when I’m just… there.\n\nI used to be more lively with clients, like chatting and laughing while I was doing hair, helping the newer stylists when they were nervous about a cut or color. And I’d go hiking with my friends and actually feel into it. Now I mostly keep my head down and try not to talk too much because I just don’t have that spark in me. Like my face probably matches that, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your walking has become slower than it used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Like, I don’t know if I’d say I’m walking like an old lady or anything, but I feel slower overall.\n\nAt work especially, my hands feel like they’re moving through glue. Even sectioning hair or mixing color, stuff I should do without thinking, takes me forever now. And then I get behind, and people notice, which makes me feel even more awkward… I used to be pretty quick with that stuff.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you been forgetting important things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um… I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like where I didn’t feel great and things felt heavy, but I don’t know if it was, like, nearly every day for two weeks in a row.

It’s hard for me to measure time like that, honestly. Sometimes a week feels like forever, and then other times I realize a month went by and I barely remember what I did besides work and come home. Like around busy salon seasons, holidays especially, everything kind of blurs together because people want their hair done before events and I’m just trying to keep up, so I don’t know if that counts or if I was just tired.

So… I guess I can’t really say yes for sure.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I came in because things have been feeling off, like I’m not keeping up the way I used to. At work, my hands feel like they’re moving through glue or something. I’m a hairdresser, so I’m usually pretty quick with sectioning hair or mixing color, like that’s just muscle memory, you know? But lately it takes me forever.\n\nAnd it’s embarrassing because people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be. Like clients don’t always say it directly, but you can tell, and coworkers… I don’t know. It makes me feel weird. I used to be good at getting through appointments without thinking so hard about every little step.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any shaking or tremor in your hands when they’re resting?\n\nPatient: Um… resting? I don’t think so. Like if my hands are just in my lap, I haven’t noticed them shaking or anything. I mean, sometimes I stare at them because I’m worried they’re not doing what I want fast enough, but that’s more when I’m working, like holding scissors or those little foils that stick to everything. But just sitting there… no, I don’t think I’ve seen a tremor.\n\nDoctor: Have your muscles been feeling stiff?\n\nPatient: Stiff… um, I’m not sure. I don’t really think of it as stiff, exactly. Like sometimes after standing all day at the salon I feel kind of tight, but that could just be normal, you know? We have those mats but they’re not as comfortable as people think, and then you end up leaning weird over the chair when someone’s hair is really long.\n\nBut muscle stiffness like… I don’t know, I haven’t really noticed that in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: When you walk, have you noticed one arm swinging less than the other?\n\nPatient: I… don’t think I’ve noticed that. Like, I don’t really watch my arms when I walk, you know? I’m usually thinking about where I’m going, or if I left my coffee somewhere at the salon, or whether I’m late.\n\nBut no one’s said anything to me about one arm not swinging, and I haven’t caught it in a mirror or anything. So… I’m not sure, but I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements in general feel slower than they used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to explain, but at work it’s like my hands are moving through glue or something. Like sectioning hair, mixing color, even folding the foils… it all takes me way longer than it used to.\n\nAnd I used to be pretty fast, you know, like that was kind of my thing. I could get into a rhythm. Now I’ll be standing there trying to part the hair straight and it’s like my hands just won’t catch up with what I’m thinking. People have noticed too—like coworkers kind of waiting on me, or clients asking if everything’s okay. So… yeah, I feel slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your handwriting getting smaller than it used to be?\n\nPatient: My handwriting? Um… I don’t know, I haven’t really noticed it getting smaller. I don’t write by hand that much anymore, honestly—mostly I’m typing appointments into the system or texting clients, and at the salon it’s all little notes on color formulas, which are already messy because I’m rushing and there’s foil everywhere and gloves on and—yeah.\n\nSo… smaller? I’m not sure. I don’t think so, but I’d have to look at old notes or something.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any stiffness or rigidity in your arms or legs?\n\nPatient: Stiffness… um, I don’t know. Like, my legs get tired if I’m standing at the salon all day, but that’s kind of normal, right? Everybody complains about that—especially when we’re booked back to back and someone’s doing a full color correction and you barely sit down.\n\nBut rigidity, like… locked up? I haven’t really noticed that. My arms and legs don’t feel stiff in a way that I can clearly point to. I guess I’m not totally sure what you mean by rigidity, but nothing obvious like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed one hand shaking when you’re using it, like while cutting hair or holding something?\n\nPatient: Um… with one hand shaking while I’m using it? I don’t think I’ve noticed that, not really.\n\nI mean, sometimes my hands feel awkward when I’m cutting or holding a brush, but I always think that’s because I’m trying to hurry, or because the client is talking and I’m trying to listen and count the sections in my head—like if I lose track of where I put the clips, then everything gets messy. But an actual shake, like one hand trembling? I can’t say I’ve seen that. If it happened, I don’t think it’s been obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I wake up and it’s like this heavy, flat sadness just sitting in my chest, and before anything even happens I’m already tired of the day. Like there’s no point trying to build the life I was planning, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd then I’ll start thinking about stupid stuff too, like how I was supposed to have a nicer apartment by now, or a better station at work, or—sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked. But yeah, depressed most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I used to actually like talking with clients, like hearing about their vacations or their kid’s dance recital or whatever, and I’d help the newer stylists figure things out when they got overwhelmed.\n\nNow I mostly just keep my head down and get through the appointment. Like, I don’t want to be rude, but I don’t have that… spark, I guess. Even hiking with my friends, which I used to love—there’s this trail we’d do where there’s a little overlook and everyone takes pictures, and now when they ask, I just don’t go. I tell myself I’m tired or busy, but really I just don’t feel much from it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like times where I felt really down or I didn’t want to deal with people, but I don’t know if it was most days for two weeks or if I’m just thinking of, like, being stressed.\n\nIt’s hard for me to separate it, because I’ll remember one thing—like being upset about work or feeling behind compared to everyone else—and then I start thinking about all these other periods in my life, and they kind of blur together. So… I don’t know. I can’t give you a clear yes or no.\n\nDoctor: Did these symptoms start after a specific stressful event in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it did start after something specific. My hours got cut at the salon, and at first I kept telling myself it was temporary, like everyone has slow weeks, you know? But then my savings started draining away, and I’d check my account and feel like I was just watching everything I worked for disappear.\n\nThat was… maybe about a year and eight months ago now? Around then is when it really started feeling like this instead of just regular stress.\n\nDoctor: When you have a day that your money situation feels less stressful, does your mood improve noticeably?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe I’ll feel a tiny bit less panicky if, like, a bill isn’t due that exact day, but the mood doesn’t really lift.\n\nMost days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness sitting in my chest, like… like there’s no point in trying to build the life I thought I was going to have. Even if the money stuff is quieter for a day, that feeling is still there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this depressed nearly every day for at least two months in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely more than two months. I mean, it’s hard to track exactly because the days kind of run together, and I’ll think, “Was that spring or last winter?” and then I start remembering random stuff from work and it gets mixed up.\n\nBut the really heavy depression… that’s been with me about a year and two months now, I think. Pretty much nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Before this current depression, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if there was a whole separate time before this like that. I remember having bad weeks, like after stuff with school or work, and I’d get really down, but this current thing feels different.\n\nMost days now I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest, like there’s no point in trying to build the life I was planning.
